instruction and the data-return confirmation from its exit plan, and is refused while any legal hold applies to that tenant (deleting the key destroys access to data Object Lock would otherwise protect). **C-16:** the codec is on for both `temporal` and `temporal-cloud` (one rule; moving between them needs no re-encryption); failure messages and stack traces are encrypted too (failure converter `encodeCommonAttributes: true`); workflow IDs, search attributes, signal/query names and task-queue names are not encoded and may contain no personal or REGULATED data. **C-17:** when a | 2026-10-26 | PL2-D-32   | `[OWNER]` Supply-chain hardening, found by the Operations Hub Semgrep run; **tightens IPB-02-33 from 72 h to 7 days**. `pnpm-workspace.yaml`: `minimumReleaseAge: 10080` (7 days), `minimumReleaseAgeExclude: ["@infrabyx/*"]`, `trustPolicy: no-downgrade`, `blockExoticSubdeps: true`; `renovate.json` `minimumReleaseAge: "7 days"`. `lefthook` is pinned to 2.2.1, because 2.2.0 and 2.2.1 carry npm provenance from GitHub Actions and 2.2.2 was published manually without it. The products require the same 7 days, so a platform release can be installed only once its third-party dependencies are at least 7 days old. Applying the policy moved the too-new pins: `turbo` 2.11.10 → 2.11.9, `typescript-eslint` 8.72.0 → 8.71.2, and the transitive `rollup` 4.65.0 → 4.64.10, `es-toolkit` 1.53.0 → 1.52.0, `fast-uri` 3.1.9 → 3.1.8, `ignore` 7.0.14 → 7.0.12. `ipb:check` fails if any of these settings or the lefthook pin regresses | 2026-10-26 | PL2-D-48   | C-11 and C-13 as built: the allow-list is ES256 and PS256 only (RS256 is not an approved C-11 algorithm; both IdPs sign ES256 per C-12). Besides the listed headers, `x5t`, `x5t#S256` and `b64` are refused. RFC 7520 §4 has no ES256 or PS256 example, so its vectors (RS256, PS384, ES512, HS256) are negative tests; RFC 7515 A.3 proves the ES256 primitive, and the `jose` differential test covers generated ES256 and PS256 tokens. Vectors come from the IETF JOSE cookbook repository and jwcrypto's copy of RFC 7515 Appendix A (rfc-editor.org is unreachable from the build sandbox), stored decoded and without private members. SNS also checks the signing certificate's validity period; Cashfree timestamps are accepted in seconds or milliseconds | 2026-10-26 | PL2-D-50   | `[OWNER]` **Field-encryption format v2**, approved as the answer to OWNER-QUESTIONS #1 and introduced before gate A in its own PR. Every KMS wrap and unwrap of a data key carries the C-14 encryption context `{ tenant_id, purpose }`. `purpose` comes from the fixed registry `KMS_PURPOSES` in `@infrabyx/contracts` (`field`, `health`; identifiers only, no personal data; extended only there). The context is canonical JSON (`encodeKmsContext` in `@infrabyx/crypto`); AWS KMS receives it as the encryption context, Vault Transit as associated data, and LocalKms as AES-GCM AAD. Every adapter refuses `generateDataKey` without a valid context (`IPB-PORT-003`), and a wrong tenant or purpose on unwrap fails closed (`IPB-KMS-001`, `integrity`). New writes use v2 only: header `[0x02][purpose][wrapped key][AES-GCM body]`, with the purpose stored and the tenant supplied by the caller. Data keys are cached per key, tenant and purpose. v1 stays readable; `FieldEnvelope.rewrapToV2` and `FieldCrypto.rewrapField` re-wrap a v1 data key under the context and leave the field ciphertext byte-for-byte unchanged. No production data exists, so nothing stored in production changes. `FieldEnvelope.seal/open` now take the context (a 0.x API change, minor) | 2026-10-26 | PL2-D-55   | `[OWNER]` **Impersonation stays off on both IDENTITY adapters** (answer to OWNER-QUESTIONS #2, 26 Oct 2026). Keycloak issues no RFC 8693 `act` claim, and Zitadel's impersonation needs the impersonator's own token, which `issueToken(claims, ttl)` does not carry; `issueToken` with an `actor` throws `IPB-PORT-001` (`unsupported`). `verifyToken` still maps an `act` claim and the kernel refuses mutations under one (`IPB-SEC-003`). | 2026-10-26 | PL2-D-60   | WORKFLOW as built: Temporal SDK 1.24.0. The C-16 codec seals each payload with the field envelope under the C-14 context `{ tenant_id: platform, purpose: workflow }` (new registered purpose, contracts minor) and AAD bound to the namespace; it refuses unencrypted payloads on decode. Failures use `DefaultFailureConverter({ encodeCommonAttributes: true })`, loaded by path in workers. `temporal-cloud` requires mTLS or an API key and takes INTERNAL inputs only (PL2-D-16); CONFIDENTIAL inputs there must be STORAGE references. Ids, names and task queues are checked to be identifiers without personal data. `cancel(id, reason)`: Temporal carries no reason, so the caller ledgers it. The Temporal Cloud TLS and API-key connection is proven only by the sandbox leg (the dev server has no TLS). Worker processes see decrypted failure messages, so their logs go through the OBSERVABILITY scrubber A third-party mirror is never used. **As implemented:** quay.io refused the anonymous pull from CI ("unauthorized", 26 Oct 2026), so the fallback is in use: CI clones tag `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, checks it is commit `07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb`, builds it with Go 1.24 and caches the binary (`scripts/ci/minio-server.sh`); `minio` is listed under "Built from source" in `infra/docker/TEST_IMAGES.md`, not in `TEST_IMAGES`. The MinIO-server leg proves presigned-URL expiry, method and content-type enforcement. `minio` stays refused outside dev and test even when its leg is green, because its upstream is frozen (`sandbox-status.json`). (2) [VERIFY] outcome for SES: moto 5.2.4 covers raw and simple sends and identities but not SES v2 templates, suppression or the account call, so the hermetic leg uses an SES v2 stub plus moto for raw send and identities. (3) Inbound webhook failures use `IPB-API-018` (IPB-10 §10.6.3), added to the catalogue; the messaging ports' `verifyWebhook` takes optional `WebhookRequestMeta` (socket source IP, query string). (4) Vendors without signatures present our C-19 secret, compared by `verifyWebhookSecret` (SHA-256 then constant time): as a bearer header (Brevo, MSG91), or as the `ipb_token` URL parameter where the vendor can only call a URL (Gupshup SMS and WhatsApp), which ingress logs must redact. Brevo also checks its published source ranges. (5) Without vendor idempotency or message search (SES, MSG91, Gupshup, Meta), a send left pending is sent again: at-least-once for messages, never for money. Brevo takes a UUID `idempotencyKey` derived from our key and is looked up by tag; MSG91 gets the key as `CRQID`. (6) Attachments: at most 10 files and 10 MiB, from GENERAL or PUBLIC_ASSETS storage only. (7) Brevo refuses a manual suppression (it would need a contact record at Brevo, beyond delivery-only); hard bounces and complaints are blocked by Brevo itself. SES `getStatus`, MSG91 and Gupshup SMS `getStatus`, Brevo `sendRaw` and Gupshup WhatsApp `uploadMedia` are declared unsupported. (8) Brevo is a failover target only for brands whose sending domain is authenticated on Brevo (checked, cached 10 minutes); a paused or suspended SES account is `IPB-PORT-002` (reputation block). (9) Gupshup SMS is called as an HTTPS POST form so the password never sits in a URL (the docs show GET); MSG91's key goes as a header, the balance check included. (10) WhatsApp: session messages are checked against the caller's last-inbound time (24 hours) and the vendor's refusal (Meta 131047, Gupshup 470) maps to the same error; marketing templates are refused while the new setting `mkt.meta.enabled` is false (OWN-08), checked by template category before every send; `getConversationCost` reads the new setting `int.whatsapp.rate_card` (configuration only, never committed); Graph API v26.0. (11) New settings `int.sms.low_balance_credits` (default 500) and `int.whatsapp.rate_card`; `WhatsappEvent` gains optional `pricingCategory` and `errorCode` | 2026-10-27 | PL2-D-65   | `[OWNER]` **OWNER-QUESTIONS #6 answered.** (1) Duplicates after an unknown outcome are not acceptable for every message. PL3 NOTIFY gives every message type a resend class: _safe to resend_ (OTPs, login and security alerts, receipts) is resent once after an unknown outcome, and delivery reports remove the duplicate where the vendor provides one; _hold for review_ (collection or recovery reminders, legal and demand notices, KFS and loan-term communications, anything that could read as harassment or a second legal notice) is never resent automatically after an unknown outcome and is queued for a person to confirm. An unclassified type defaults to hold for review. Until PL3 the adapters keep the PL2-D-64 behaviour, which is acceptable because nothing is sent for real. (2) The Gupshup URL secret is accepted as interim, on conditions now built: the `ipb_token` must be at least 32 random bytes (43 base64url characters; shorter is refused) and is rotated like other webhook secrets (C-19); the telemetry scrubber redacts credential-named query parameters (`ipb_token`, `*token`, `*secret`, `api_key`, `key`, `sig`, `signature`, `password`) from every log, span and error attribute; the source-IP allow-list applies as soon as Gupshup publishes ranges; and a state-changing event is passed on only after its status is confirmed. Gupshup documents no status-lookup API yet [VERIFY in the sandbox], so `confirmStatus` is injected and without it Gupshup events are acknowledged but withheld. Revisit once the vendor test accounts exist; both Gupshup adapters stay unproven until then. (3) Brevo is used for sending only: no contact records and no manual blocks (OWN-15); the PL3 NOTIFY suppression list is the single source of truth | 2026-10-27 | PL2-D-66   | **PL2 session 7: PAYMENTS adapters `razorpay` and `cashfree`, test mode only, on the approved locked list P-01 to P-18.** Vendor facts come from the vendors' official SDKs (razorpay-node 2.9.8; cashfree-pg-sdk-nodejs 6.0.6 and the Go SDK generated from Cashfree's OpenAPI spec); unproven details are marked [VERIFY] for the sandbox legs and both adapters stay `unproven`. Implementation choices within the approved items: (1) P-03: the env guard refuses any `rzp_live_` value outside prod/dr; the adapters refuse a live credential (Razorpay live key id, Cashfree production host) outside prod/dr and a test one inside them except with `healthProbe`. (2) P-04: Cashfree amounts are written as exact decimal text in the JSON body (no float), and amounts read back must convert exactly or the call fails. (3) P-05: Razorpay `receipt`, Cashfree `order_id`, `refund_id` and `subscription_id` are derived from the idempotency key; Cashfree also gets a UUID `x-idempotency-key` (422 → `IPB-PORT-004`; 409 on create returns the existing order). Lookup: Razorpay orders by receipt; Cashfree orders and mandates by our id, where only a 404 counts as absent. Refunds (both) and Razorpay mandates cannot be searched by our reference, so lookup answers `unknown` and the router sends them to the human queue (P-07). (4) P-07: the refund approval check reads the approval through a `RefundApprovals` view (the executor can pass the record it receives) and requires action `payments.refund`, state `approved`, no open delay and the canonical digest of `{ paymentId, amountMinor, reason }`; at execution the PA's own refund list is read first and a refund with our reference is returned, never repeated; the amount is capped at captured minus earlier refunds. Cashfree refunds by order, so `paymentId` is the order id there. (5) P-08: router method specs gain `noFailover` (refund, createMandate, capture, createCheckoutSession, verifySignature) and `router.portFor(port, adapter)` pins a call to the PA that owns the order. (6) P-09: Cashfree webhooks are de-duplicated on `x-idempotency-header` when present (webhook version ≥ 2025-01-01), else on a hash of the raw body; Razorpay on `X-Razorpay-Event-Id`. (7) Cashfree requires the customer's phone on orders (and email on mandates): a `customerContact(customerRef)` resolver supplies them to this IN, REGULATED adapter only. (8) Cashfree `x-api-version` defaults to 2023-08-01 [VERIFY the current supported version]; Razorpay mandates use `total_count` 120 [VERIFY]. (9) P-18 manifests in `packages/connectors/marketplace/` with a lint test; `PaymentEvent.method` (category only, P-10) | 2026-10-27 | PL3-D-02   | **Patch `@infrabyx/connectors` 0.1.1 and a consumer smoke check.** A product's platform-compat run found that `@infrabyx/connectors/workflow` and `./workflow/worker` fail to load in plain Node ESM: `@temporalio/proto` is CommonJS whose exports Node cannot detect, so the named import `{ temporal }` failed (vitest's interop hid it). The codec now uses the default import. The other vendor SDK imports were checked by importing every export from the packed tarballs; none has the same fault. New check `consumer-smoke` (`scripts/consumer-smoke.mjs`, job in `ci.yml`, step before publish in `release.yml`): `pnpm pack` every publishable package, install the tarballs with plain npm in a clean directory (each `@infrabyx` dependency overridden to its tarball, peer dependencies including optional ones installed at their declared range), and import every JavaScript export with plain Node 24.22.1 in its own process; directory exports (keys ending in `/`) are skipped, wildcard exports expanded against the installed files, JSON imported with `{ type: "json" }`, other file types listed and skipped; any error fails. Added to the target ruleset's required checks | 2026-10-27 | PL3-D-14   | **OWNER-QUESTIONS #17 closed: registry checksums read by CI.** `registry-dist.yml` run 1 (27 Oct 2026) read `npm view <name>@<version> dist` from GitHub Packages for the eight PL2 gate A versions. `contracts`, `crypto`, `config`, `audit` and `connectors` match the reproduced values in `PL2-gate-A.md` §6. **`tenancy`, `seed` and `testing` 0.2.0 differ**: the CI-built tarballs are not byte-identical to a local `pnpm pack` at `a09d13c`. The report and STATUS now carry the registry values (the registry is authoritative; products verify against it). Cause, found by rebuilding `a09d13c` (Node 24.22.1, frozen lockfile): (a) the bin targets of `tenancy`, `seed` and `testing` are packed 0755 or 0644 depending on whether `pnpm install` linked them after `dist` existed, and the gate-A reproduction had them 0644 (a fresh install-then-build gives the registry's 0755 and reproduces `tenancy` and `testing` exactly); (b) `pnpm pack` writes the rewritten `workspace:*` dependencies into `package.json` in a non-deterministic order, so two packs of `seed` from the same tree differ in key order only. File contents are otherwise identical. Tarballs are therefore reproducible in content, not byte for byte; the registry values are the reference, and `registry-dist.yml` (manual) gains a `compare_ref` mode that rebuilds at a commit and diffs each tarball against the registry's. No published version changes (3) `json-rules-engine` 7.3.1 (ISC). (4) PUSH `expo`: the Expo push HTTP API v2 (`/--/api/v2/push/send`, 100 messages a chunk; `/push/getReceipts`, 300 ids; optional bearer access token; gzip), called with `fetch` through the PL2 HTTP core, no SDK. (5) PUSH `fcm`: the HTTP v1 API needs an OAuth 2.0 token minted from a service-account RS256 JWT, which is not an approved C-item; default: the adapter takes the access token from SECRETS (minted outside the platform, for example by workload identity federation) and signs nothing (#18). (6) ESIGN `leegality`: webhooks carry `mac` = HMAC-SHA1(document id, private salt), not an approved C-item; default: a Leegality webhook is an untrusted trigger and the adapter acts only on the status it then reads from the Leegality API (#19). (7) ESIGN `digio`: HTTP Basic (client id and secret from SECRETS), document upload and status under `/v2/client/document/*`; no webhook signature scheme found, so the same read-back rule applies. | 2026-10-27 | PL3-C-01   | `[OWNER]` **OWNER-QUESTIONS #18 answered: C-24 approved, RS256 for vendor-dictated signing only.** RS256 (RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 with SHA-256) is used only to sign the Google service-account JWT assertion that FCM HTTP v1's OAuth 2.0 token exchange requires; never for platform tokens, where C-12 (ES256) stays the rule. The service-account key is read through SECRETS; signing happens inside `@infrabyx/crypto` with `node:crypto` (crypto stays the single call-site, `ipb/no-direct-crypto`); the key and the assertion never appear in logs, spans or errors. The access token is cached until shortly before its expiry (refresh 5 minutes early). Keyless workload identity federation is preferred once the platform runs on a cloud that supports it. Built in PL3 session 4 with the `fcm` adapter, which replaces the PL3-D-15 default (a SECRETS-held token minted outside the platform) | 2026-10-27 | PL3-C-02   | `[OWNER]` **OWNER-QUESTIONS #19 answered: C-25 approved, HMAC-SHA1 for verifying Leegality webhooks only.** HMAC-SHA1 remains a sound MAC (SHA-1's collision weakness does not carry over to HMAC), so it is accepted for verification of Leegality webhooks and nothing else; it is never used to sign. Conditions: constant-time comparison over the exact bytes Leegality signs (raw body or the documented field), a timestamp or replay window wherever Leegality sends one, and the document status read back from the Leegality API before acting on any state-changing webhook (the PL3-D-15 read-back stays). When the test account exists, ask Leegality for SHA-256 signing and switch if offered. Built in PL3 session 6 in `@infrabyx/crypto` (C-13 vendor list) Dates are `YYYY-MM-DD` (strings in CEL, epoch days in ZEN and json-rules); numbers in plain notation; string literals without quote, backslash or control characters; field names that are engine keywords or JavaScript object members are refused. Engines decide only which rows match; outputs are assembled from the table: `first` takes the lowest matching row, `collect` returns each output as the list of matched rows' values in row order. Inputs are checked before any engine runs (every declared input with its type, nothing undeclared, at most 64 KiB); an invalid input is `UNDETERMINED`/`input_invalid`. A differential test runs 600 generated inputs through all three. NOTIFY hands outbound withdrawals (unsubscribe link) to the product through `onConsentChanged` with the Ecosystem data, and the unkeyed hash is computed only at that boundary. (4) **Snapshot:** one REPEATABLE READ read-only transaction (new `MutationKernel.read` option `snapshot`) reads columns with their `dataclass:` comment, rows (`tenant_id = $1`, or `id = $1` for the tenant row), single-column foreign keys between exported fields (→ `relationships.csv`), then recounts each table; a difference fails the job (`IPB-EXP-003`). (5) **Service:** only user principals with `ORG_OWNER` (plus the `roles` option) request and download (`IPB-EXP-005`); 5 concurrent and 20 a day per principal (`IPB-SEC-019`, counted in `export.export_job`); restricted folders `IPB-EXP-001`; scopes `FULL_TENANT`, `DATASET` (map entity names in `ref`) and `AUDIT_PACK`, other scopes and any destination but download `IPB-EXP-002`; the ZIP goes to STORAGE in bucket class `CONFIDENTIAL` (there is no EXPORT class), written with `put` (no Object Lock; retention defaults, PL3-D-10); the predecessor is the tenant's last archive of the same scope; downloads presign for 300 s and ledger `<archive id>:<sha256>` as the resource id, so the hash is in the ledger itself. | 2026-10-27 | PL3-D-23   | `[ASSUMED – owner review]` **PL3 session 6 as built: ESIGN `leegality` and `digio`, and C-25.** (1) **C-25** `verifyLeegalityMac` in `@infrabyx/crypto`: HMAC-SHA1 of the document id with the private salt, hex, constant-time comparison; when the body carries `timestamp` (or `createdAt`; seconds, milliseconds or ISO 8601), it must be within ±5 minutes; `hmac-sha1` is in the registry for Leegality verification only. The platform never computes HMAC-SHA1 for its own use: the Leegality stub uses the openssl CLI. (2) **Vendor shapes** come from search summaries because the vendors' documentation hosts are unreachable from the build sandbox; every endpoint, field and status name is [VERIFY] until the sandbox legs (#55, #56). Leegality: `X-Auth-Token`; `POST /v3.1/sign/request` with `profileId`, a base64 PDF and invitees, returning `documentId` and one `signUrl` per invitation; `GET /v3.2/sign/request` (status) and `GET /v3.0/document/details` (signed files); `DELETE /v3.0/sign/request/invitation` (cancel each unsigned invitation); `status: 0` on HTTP 200 is a refusal. Digio: HTTP Basic; `POST /v2/client/document/uploadpdf`, `GET /v2/client/document/{id}`, `GET /v2/client/document/download`, `POST /v2/client/document/{id}/cancel`; a signer has one identifier (e-mail, else phone). (3) **No downgrade:** Leegality signature types come from its workflows, so each method maps to one configured `profileId`, a method without a profile is refused, and an envelope uses one workflow (all signers one method); signature positions come from the workflow, so non-empty `fields` are refused. Digio maps Aadhaar OTP → `aadhaar`, OTP e-acceptance → `electronic`, DSC → `dsc`; biometric Aadhaar, account eSign and wet ink are refused. Sessions (`aadhaarEsign`, `dscSign`) are refused for a signer created with another method. (4) **Webhooks:** Leegality by C-25; Digio by our C-19 URL secret (`ipb_token`), as for Gupshup (PL2-D-65), because Digio documents no signature. Both then read the envelope back and return that status only; unknown documents are refused before any read-back. (5) **Envelopes:** `esr_` ids, deterministic from the idempotency key when one is given (one envelope per key); envelope ids, vendor references and each signer's method and vendor handle live in an `EsignEnvelopeStore` that the product persists (contracts interface; `MemoryEsignEnvelopeStore` in testing); no platform table, since no PL3 package owns signing workflows. (6) **Documents:** read from STORAGE with the SHA-256 checked and a PDF header required; the signed PDF is written to STORAGE `REGULATED` under `tenant/<id>/esign/`; signature records name the ESP and the vendor document (`esp:<vendor>:<method>`, audit-trail reference). `verifySignature` is `unsupported` on both adapters (no PAdES validator yet). (7) Both adapters are India-hosted (`IN`), `maxDataClass` REGULATED, sandbox mode by default, `unproven` in `sandbox-status.json` with issues #55 and #56; `ESIGN` joins the port-contract matrix. Settings `int.esign.provider` and `int.esign.expire_in_days` (Digio `expire_in_days`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | PL3 plan §8, §9.5 (C-25); This deletion rule is the owner's approval of a locked-list item (data deletion), limited to export archives. (3) **#24 time zone:** data stays in UTC with ISO 8601 offsets; the archive README states this and shows the export time in IST (`istTime`, UTC+05:30). Because the README is archive content, the reference map moves to `REF-EXPORT-1.1` (minor; `maps/REF-EXPORT-1.0.json` kept for the 90-day dual run) and the golden archive is regenerated under `golden/REF-EXPORT-1.1/`. (4) **#24 roles:** `ORG_OWNER` exports directly (`request`, R0). `ORG_ADMIN` calls `submit`, which raises an R2 approval `export.job.request` whose only checker role is `ORG_OWNER`; the executor records the job in the approving transaction (`export_job.approval_id`, requester = the ORG_ADMIN), and `sweep` enqueues approved jobs not started after one minute (no after-commit hook exists in the kernel). The requesting ORG_ADMIN may download the archive of their own approved job; ORG_OWNER any archive of the tenant. (5) **SVC_EXPORT_SWEEP** (`svc_export_sweep`) reads `export.export_job` (id, tenant, state, created_at), `export.archive` (id, tenant, created_at, expires_at) and `export.archive_deletion` (archive_id, tenant) across tenants through `ServiceReader`, ledgered per tenant; no write grant. (6) **#24 DPDP:** data-principal access requests are a separate product flow, not the tenant export; nothing in `@infrabyx/export` serves them. (7) **#25 ESIGN:** confirmed: products keep signing records and evidence (`EsignEnvelopeStore`); the platform keeps only what the adapters need for idempotency and status. (3) **Output classifier:** new PII, echoed hidden context (system-prompt sentences of 40+ characters, the data instruction), citations outside the data blocks, reserved advice; `redact` (default) or `refuse` (`blocked: true`, `IPB-AI-042`, "200 with blocked"). (5) **Structured output:** `z.fromJSONSchema` validation, one repair turn on the same model, then `IPB-AI-041`. (6) **Prompts and evals:** three platform prompts (`ai.grounded_answer@1` REGULATED with 110 cases, `ai.summarise@1`, `ai.extract_fields@1`); the Vidhi persona and plain-language prompts come with `vidhi` (session 6). CI's model is a scripted fake whose replies go through the gateway's own text pipeline, so the gate proves the harness, graders and guards; results are committed per version and must match a re-run. Prompt and eval files are outside Prettier because their content is hashed. `prompts sync` activates a version only with a current passing result, and in staging and above only with a live result (#35). (7) **Retrieval:** `ai/0003` enables `vector`; supported dimensions 256, 384, 512, 768, 1024 and 1536, each with a partial HNSW index; embeddings and `tsv` are built from scrubbed text and the original is sealed with FieldCrypto (always at least CONFIDENTIAL); search uses iterative HNSW scans and RRF (k = 60); no RERANK (#28); SECRET text is never indexed. | 2026-10-27 | PL4-D-09 | `[ASSUMED – owner review]` **PL4 session 7a as built: scheduled tasks and missions.** (1) **Recurrence:** the in-house RFC 5545 subset (A-18) in the schedule's IANA zone: DTSTART;TZID, RRULE FREQ DAILY/WEEKLY/MONTHLY with INTERVAL, BYDAY (plain weekdays), BYHOUR, BYMINUTE, BYMONTHDAY (negative allowed), COUNT or UNTIL, WKST=MO only, and EXDATE;TZID. Anything else is refused at save, as is a DTSTART that is not itself an occurrence and a rule that fires more than 24 times a day. DST follows RFC 5545 §3.3.5 (a gap moves forward; an ambiguous time is the first instant); tested in New York and London. (2) **Due runs:** `Scheduler.tick` is vidhi's own poller, reading only id, tenant, state, kind and next run time across tenants as the new service reader `SVC_VIDHI_SCHED` (ledgered per tenant), rather than a new `workflow` timer kind, so `workflow` does not depend on `vidhi`; products drive `tick` once a minute from their QUEUE or cron. `vidhi.schedule_run` (unique per schedule and occurrence or event id) makes every occurrence run at most once. (3) **Re-check (IPB-03-65):** scheduling on for the tenant; the owner an active member who still holds **every** role recorded when they created the schedule (`memberStatus` in `tenancy`), plus the product's optional permission check; plan entitlement; the skill loaded, enabled and `sched_allowed`; no kill switch; connector tokens valid. A failure is `IPB-VID-020` with a reason (NOTIFY `vidhi.schedule.skipped`, event `ScheduleRunSkipped`); the third consecutive skip (`vidhi.sched.max_skips`) pauses it with `IPB-VID-021` (NOTIFY `vidhi.schedule.paused`, mandatory). Only the owner or an `ORG_ADMIN` resumes it. Agents cannot create or resume schedules (`IPB-VID-012`). Runs act as the agent principal `agt_vidhi_sched` for the owner; the level cap of IPB-03-67 is `Autonomy.effectiveLevel`'s. A crash between the scheduling commit and the task's creation leaves a `started` run without a task (visible, not repeated). (4) **Event triggers:** a CloudEvents type plus an optional CEL condition evaluated by the rules engine over `{type, data}`; a condition that does not parse is refused at save; the event id keys the run. (5) **Missions:** the planner is `vidhi.mission_plan@1` (`structured`, 41-case golden set); steps outside the scope are dropped and an empty plan refused; the mission level is the lowest ceiling of its skills and caps every task in it (`maxLevel`); the budget (tokens, cost, wall-clock, tool calls) is checked before every step and when a step waits, and running out stops it with `IPB-VID-070`, cancelling the step in flight; a step that needs a human makes the mission WAITING until a later `advance`; a rejected or failed step fails it. Missions are a resumable state machine with a 120-second lease, so two workers never run the same step. Only skill keys, task ids and counters are stored. `@IPB-03-AT-03` passes on fakes; no provider was called | IPB-03-63 to 03-67, §03.4.7, §03.4.9; PL4 plan §8.5, §8.6, A-10, A-18 |

| 2026-10-27 | PL5-D-06 | `[ASSUMED – owner review]` **PL5 session 4 as built: `@infrabyx/cms` core.** (1) **Legal review returns to `in_review`.** `@IPB-04-AT-03` needs a separate checker approval after the legal reviewer, so `legal_approve` moves `legal_review → in_review` (with the legal sign-off recorded), and `approve` then moves to `approved`; IPB-04 §04.4.3 draws `legal_approve → approved` (PR-034). The checker and the legal reviewer of one approval are always different people, and neither is the maker, which is stricter than §04.4.3's "the same person only if a second editor has approved". `approve` and `publish_now` without the legal sign-off are `IPB-CMS-005`. (2) **Agents and the platform scope.** IPB-02-11 opens the platform scope only to non-agent PLT principals, so in the platform CMS an agent cannot even save a draft (the kernel refuses with `IPB-SEC-005`); the state machine still limits agents to edit and submit (`IPB-SEC-004`) for defence in depth. A product's drafting skill proposes content that an editor saves. (3) **Service principals.** The scheduler (`svc_cms_scheduler`) and the purge (`svc_cms_purge`) run in the platform context with `PLT_CONTENT_EDITOR` (the only way IPB-02-11 admits a platform write); they never pass the Studio's role checks and only run the scheduled publish and unpublish and the purge, each ledgered with the service as actor. No `SVC_CMS_SCHEDULER` reader is needed (the plan's §8 entry), because content is platform rows, not cross-tenant reads. (4) **One table for content.** Menus, footers and offices are content types in `cms.content_item` with revisions, rather than separate `nav_item` and `office` tables (plan §12), so they share the state machine, maker-checker and the purge. An office is deleted only through maker-checker (IPB-04-72). (5) **Slugs and paths.** The slug a revision publishes under is stored on the revision; the item keeps the served `published_path`, so a draft can change its slug while the old path keeps serving. Publishing at a new path adds a 301 from the old one and repoints earlier redirects (no chain). (6) **Redirects** are a legal-review type (IPB-04-06): manual and imported redirects stay pending until a checker and the legal reviewer approve; slug-change and retire redirects are active at once, because the publish or unpublish that creates them was itself approved. Hit counts are flushed in batches with one ledger entry per flush. (7) **Claims lint.** The word list is `claims/words.json` (products add, never remove). Category claims are matched as claim phrases ("India's first", "the only", "No. 1", "market leader") rather than bare words, so "the first step" passes; they need an active category claim of class E2 or above on the item. Certification words need an active certification or audit claim of class E3 or above linked to a `vad_` document (`IPB-TRUST-006`). The payload codec (C-16) seals every payload with the KMS in use (`FieldEnvelope`, key `WORKFLOW_CODEC_KEY_ID`, default `local:workflow`, which the fixture KMS accepts; a product sets the namespace's key). (2) **The timers:** the approvals engine is now built by `createWorkflow` of `@infrabyx/workflow`, so the Work Inbox, the approval reminders, escalation and expiry exist in the reference app as in a product; the PostgreSQL poller always runs in the worker (every minute, the reconciler), and `TemporalTimers` fires on time on the task queue `<WORKFLOW_TASK_QUEUE_PREFIX>timers` (`ipb-timers`) when Temporal is configured. (3) **The worker** runs `createIpbWorker` on that queue with the `ipbTimer` workflow and the `ipbTimerFire` activity (`WorkflowTimers.fireById`, in the timer's own tenant); the helper is imported in the worker only, since it loads the Temporal native bridge; no Worker Versioning build id yet (the deployment's digest, PL6 session 6). (4) **Readiness:** WORKFLOW joins the probes as a non-critical port (`degraded`, not `down`). (5) **Compose:** the api and worker take the stack's Temporal (`127.0.0.1:57233`, namespace `default`) and depend on it; the compose job's `ready` check now covers it | The product pattern of PL3 (timers on Temporal with the poller as reconciler) is exercised end to end by the reference app; every choice is an environment variable with a local default, reversible without a migration | IPB-01 §01.4.4.2 WORKFLOW, §01.4.8.2; PL3 plan §5.1; PL2-C-04, C-16 |
